WARNINGS ABOUT THE INSTALL SECRET
  A warning carries no error number & never halts anything. Each says what happened & what was done about it.
  The kernel raises these, from Resources/Kernel/kernel.php. The description of 31020 says how root keeps the install secret, in two
  copies with the same key, & which copy the web server account reads first. A key is in use only when that account reads it.
  Root raises the first group, as every command run as root settles the secret.
    WARNING!!! The web server account <ACCOUNT> or the group of the same name could not be looked up, so only a copy of the install secret root owns counts, & Secrets cannot be judged.
      The kernel names the web server account www-data, & this host has no account or no group of that name. A copy that account owns
      is not taken, & the group of Secrets & of each copy is not judged, so no copy is written again on every command for a group
      nobody here can name. A web server that runs as another account cannot read the key.
    WARNING!!! The install secret was settled without its lock, so another command run as root could have been settling it at the same moment. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      One command run as root settles the secret at a time, under secret.lock in Engine, so a command that read the old key never writes
      it back over a new rotation. What follows says why the lock was not held. Engine is not root's own yet, another command held the
      lock for fifteen seconds, or the filesystem cannot lock. Where Engine does not exist, as before the first -fp, no lock is taken &
      nothing is said, because nothing else can be settling a secret there yet.
    WARNING!!! The copy of the install secret at <FILE_PATH> is not used. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      A copy was there & could not be used, so the other was looked in, & a new key is made only where neither can be used. What
      follows says why. A copy another account could have written, that is not a plain file, that has a second name or that an account
      but its owner may write is never read, because a key another account could have written is one it may know. One root read that
      this core cannot use carries another version, or something this application did not write. It is written again with the key in use.
    WARNING!!! <WRITE_FAILURE> The copy at <FILE_PATH> still carries the key, & the next command run as root tries again.
    WARNING!!! <WRITE_FAILURE> It carries the key in use, & the next command run as root tries again.
      A copy that carries the key in use could not be written again in the shape root keeps it, root:www-data 0640. The first is the
      copy read first, & the second the other copy. The key is unchanged, so every session is unaffected.
    WARNING!!! <WRITE_FAILURE> It was not known to carry the key in use, so it was removed. The copy at <FILE_PATH> carries the key.
    WARNING!!! <WRITE_FAILURE> It is not known to carry the key in use, & is left while the copy at <FILE_PATH> is not right.
      The other copy carries another key, none, or one root would not read, & could not be written. Once the web server account reads
      the key in the copy it reads first, the other is removed, so no other key can ever be found there. Until then it is left, because
      that account may be reading it, & an error after it says when that account would not read the key root holds.
    WARNING!!! <WRITE_FAILURE> Nothing was at that name, so no other key can be found there.
      The other copy was missing & could not be made. The copy read first carries the key, so the web server account reads it. From
      v4.3.1 this ends with its own sentence. WRITE_FAILURE in each of these is the second, third or sixth message of 31020 without its
      last sentence, about the copy it names.
    WARNING!!! <ADDITIONAL_DATA> The web server account cannot read the install secret until it is.
      A new copy was written as root & could not be given the web server account's group. What comes first says why, & names the staged
      copy. Root reads the key, so the copy is still written, unless the name held a copy that account reads now, which is never replaced
      by one it could not read, & that is 31020 instead. Run  sudo php <entry point> -fp  again.
    WARNING!!! The staged copy of the install secret at <FILE_PATH> could not be removed. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      Each write makes the new copy beside the secret under a name of its own, & renames it over the secret's once it is whole. This
      one did not take the name & could not be removed. It may carry a key, & is root's, 0640, in a folder root holds. Remove it as root.
      It never stops the next write, which makes a name of its own.
    WARNING!!! The install secret at <FILE_PATH> was rewritten as root with its existing key. Every session is unaffected.
    WARNING!!! The install secret at <FILE_PATH> was copied to <FILE_PATH> with its existing key. Every session is unaffected.
    WARNING!!! The copy of the install secret at <FILE_PATH> carried another key, & now carries the one at <FILE_PATH>. Every session derived from its key is now invalid.
      A copy was not in the shape root keeps it, & was written again. The first carried the key, & was not root's or not 0640, as an
      older release or an old chown -R leaves it. The second was missing. The third carried another key, so a visitor whose session was
      derived from it starts a new one.
    WARNING!!! A new install secret was written to <FILE_PATHS>.
      No usable copy was found, so root made a key. The first command run as root on an installation does this, & so does a data
      location that lost its secret. Every session derived from an earlier key is invalid.
    WARNING!!! The install secret was rotated. Every session derived from the old one is now invalid.
      --rotate-secret wrote a new key into the copy the web server account reads first. A warning or an error before it names any copy
      that could not be written.
    WARNING!!! The web server account reads the install secret at <FILE_PATH> first, because <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      Secrets in the data location is not ready, so the copy at the top of the data location, where older releases keep it, is read
      first. What follows says why. Secrets is not a folder, its group cannot be judged, it is not root:www-data 0750, or another account
      could replace it. -fp gives the folder its role & copies the secret in.
  The web server account raises these, as it reads the install secret. It never writes it.
    WARNING!!! A copy of the install secret is not used, because another account could have written it. Run -fp as root, which names it & why.
      The web server account judges each copy as root does, before it reads it, & passes over one root would refuse. It names no path,
      because a halt at boot shows its words to the visitor. -fp names the copy & what is wrong with it, & writes it again.
    WARNING!!! The install secret cannot be used by <ACCOUNT> & is not replaced from a web request. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      A copy that account read carries no key, another version or something that did not parse. What follows is the command that
      replaces it.
  These are raised as an account reads a secret, the install secret for the web server account & its own for any other account.
    WARNING!!! The secret file at <FILE_PATH> exists but cannot be read by <ACCOUNT>. It was almost certainly created by a different account. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      For the web server account it is a copy of the install secret that is root's & not in its group, & what follows says to run -fp as
      root. For any other account it is its own secret, in a folder another account made, & what follows says to delete it as root.
    WARNING!!! The secret file at <FILE_PATH> reports version <VERSION> & this core requires v<VERSION>. It is being replaced & every session derived from it is now invalid.
    WARNING!!! The secret file at <FILE_PATH> emitted <NUMBER> byte(s) instead of parsing as PHP. It is malformed & is being replaced.
      A per user secret this core cannot use is replaced with a new key. A file that reports no version predates version pinning. Until

    WARNING!!! The secret file written to <FILE_PATH> did not read back correctly & was removed.

    WARNING!!! The per user secret folder at <FOLDER> is a link & was not used. Delete it as root & run the command again.

    WARNING!!! The per user secret folder at <FOLDER> can be reached by other accounts & was not used. Delete it as root & run the command again.

  limited. ABOUT_RESOURCE_RATE_LIMITING says how a limit is chosen.
    WARNING!!! systemd-run is not installed. Per conversion limits fall back to scheduling priority only.
    WARNING!!! A resource scope could not be created. <ADDITIONAL_DATA> Per conversion limits fall back to scheduling priority alone.

      kernel does not delegate cgroup controllers, which many NAS & appliance kernels do not & nothing here can change.
    WARNING!!! Per conversion limits are using a SYSTEM scope. This account holds systemd manage-units, which is enough to start a unit as root. Enable lingering for <ACCOUNT> with the -fp argument & remove that permission.
      The scope works on the system bus, because the web server account was given systemd's manage-units. That is enough to start a
      transient service as root, so the account that parses uploaded files has a route to root. Remove the permission.
    WARNING!!! config.php names no per conversion ceiling for <TYPE>. Using this core's built-in <LIMIT> rather than the general default, which is too small for that type. Add a <TYPE> entry to --Maximum Per Conversion Resources-- to set this yourself.
    WARNING!!! The per conversion limit for <TYPE> could not be read from <SOURCE>. Using the configured default.

    WARNING!!! The <PROFILE> sandbox profile asks for an address space limit & prlimit is not installed, so its conversion runs without one. util-linux provides prlimit.
      From v4.3.2, a profile setting AddressSpaceLimit is held to the memory its type is allowed, through prlimit, & the conversion runs
      without that ceiling where prlimit is missing, because a limit never refuses a conversion. Install util-linux.
    WARNING!!! Per conversion resource limits could not be fully enabled. <NUMBER> step(s) completed.
      -fp gives the web server account a systemd manager of its own & the cgroup controllers a scope needs, & a step did not complete.
      Root's log says which. Conversions fall back to scheduling priority until it does.
    WARNING!!! This kernel delegates no cgroup controllers, so per conversion limits fall back to scheduling priority.
      -fp would give the web server account a systemd manager of its own, & this kernel gives it no controllers to limit with. Many

  names in $EngineApplicationLayout are copied, into a folder beside Application only root can enter, & renamed into place.
  The installation it replaces is moved aside first, & nothing of it is removed before the new one is in place, from v4.3.2.
    WARNING!!! The folder the application lives in, <FOLDER>, can be changed by an account other than root, so nothing was installed. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
    WARNING!!! The running tree at <FOLDER> can be changed by an account other than root, so it was not installed. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      Root installs only a tree it trusts, into a folder only root can change, & what follows names what is in the way. The first is
      the data location, which -fp gives back to root. The second is the release the command ran from.
    WARNING!!! The tree at <FOLDER> lacks <NUMBER> file(s) of code the installation at <FOLDER> holds, <FILE_PATH> among them, so it was not installed. A drop-in holds only the files that changed. Unpack it into <FOLDER> & run -fp there.
      A drop-in is not a release. Every PHP file the installation holds in Resources & UI must be in the tree that replaces it, so a
      drop-in unpacked where older releases went is never installed in place of the whole application. A release that drops code on
      purpose installs once the installation it replaces has been moved aside by hand.
    WARNING!!! Part of the installation at <FOLDER> could not be read, so the tree at <FOLDER> was compared with the rest of it.

      Application in the data location is a file or a link. Remove it as root & run -fp again.

    WARNING!!! The running tree was not installed. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
    WARNING!!! The running tree holds no <ENTRY_POINT>, so it was not installed.
      The staged copy could not be made, an entry could not be copied, as what follows says, or the copy lacks the entry point. The
      staged copy is removed & the installation is unchanged. A full disk or a read only mount does the first two.
    WARNING!!! What an earlier installation or update left at <FOLDER> could not be removed, so the installation at <FOLDER> was not moved aside. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      An installation the backup cannot hold waits at Application.old until the new one is in place, & an update moves the one it
      replaces to the same name, so a run of either that stopped part way can leave one there. It is removed before the installation
      is moved there, & what follows says why it could not be. Nothing was moved or removed, so the installation is unchanged. Remove
      Application.old by hand & run -fp again.
    WARNING!!! The installation at <FOLDER> could not be moved aside, so the new one was not put in its place.
      The installation is moved to the backup location before the new one takes its name, or to Application.old where the backup
      cannot hold it. Neither could be done, so the installation is unchanged. An Application that resolves to a system folder is
      never moved.
    WARNING!!! The staged installation could not be moved into <FOLDER>, so the installation it would have replaced was put back.
      The installation had been moved aside & the new copy could not take its name, so the installation was moved back. It is
      unchanged, & the staged copy is removed. The tree the command ran from is unchanged too, so -fp can be run from it again.
      Until v4.3.2 an installation the backup could not hold was removed before the new copy was moved, & this left Application
      missing.
    WARNING!!! The staged installation could not be moved into <FOLDER>, & the installation it would have replaced could not be put back from <FOLDER>. Move it back by hand.
      Neither the new copy nor the installation could take the name, so Application is missing. Nothing of the installation was
      removed. It is whole in the folder named, & moving that folder to Application as root puts it back. The staged copy is removed.
    WARNING!!! The staged installation could not be moved into <FOLDER>.
      Application held nothing, so there was nothing to put back. The staged copy is removed, & the tree the command ran from is
      unchanged.
    WARNING!!! The installation at <FOLDER> is in place, & its mode could not be set to 0755. The installed copy's -fp sets it.
      The new installation has its name & is installed. The -fp the installed copy runs next gives every folder in it its mode.
      Until v4.3.2 this was reported as a staged installation that could not be moved, though it had been.
    WARNING!!! The installation it replaced is left at <FOLDER>, & could not be removed. <ADDITIONAL_DATA> Remove it by hand.
      The new installation is in place. The one it replaced waited at Application.old because the backup could not hold it, & what
      follows says why it could not be removed. Nothing runs from it.
    WARNING!!! The hosted location reported <STATE>. <ADDITIONAL_DATA>
      The hosted location is the folder the web server serves, which holds the loader in place of the entry point, the protection page,
      the static files a browser fetches & DATA. <STATE> is FAILED where a name could not be written, REFUSED where it is a link, a
      system folder or one another account could replace, or overlaps the installation, UNINSTALLED where its entry point belongs to a
      release that is not installed, & STRAY where names in it are not the installation's. What follows says what to do.

WARNINGS ABOUT SERVED LOCATIONS
  A warning carries no error number & never halts anything. Each says what happened & what was done about it.
  The Environment Core raises these, from Resources/Engine/Cores/environmentCore.php. A served location, such as DATA, is inside the
  folder the web server serves, & holds what a visitor downloads. Its protection file & the server configuration make the web server
  hand everything in it over as a download, & -v proves it with a probe. ABOUT_DATA_DIRECTORY_PROTECTION says how.
    WARNING!!! No served location is set inside the hosted location, so no protection file was kept current.
    WARNING!!! No served location is set inside the hosted location, so no protection file could be checked.
    WARNING!!! The exposure check could not run, because no served location is set inside the hosted location.
    WARNING!!! A served location is not set, so its protection file could not be checked.

    WARNING!!! ImageMagick would not report its configuration path, so <FOLDER> was located by search. Confirm it is the directory this build actually reads.
      The policy the application writes for ImageMagick goes where ImageMagick reads its configuration. ImageMagick did not say where
      that is, so the folder was found by looking. A policy written where this build does not read does nothing.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------


----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WARNINGS ABOUT THE STARTER
  A warning carries no error number & never halts anything. Each says what happened & what was done about it.
  The Starter raises these, from app.php. Each is from v4.3.2.
    WARNING!!! The application argument <COMMAND> is not one the Starter accepts.
      An argument beginning --app is the application's, & the kernel hands it to the Starter's handler. The Starter has one
      command, --app-hello, so any other is refused with exit status 2 & changes nothing. php app.php -h lists it.
    WARNING!!! A web request was refused, because this application has no web interface.
      The Starter answers the command line only. A request that reaches app.php through a web server is answered with status 403
      & one sentence of plain text, & nothing is admitted, so no session folder is made & nothing is swept for it.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
